# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent relation-map review - round 13

Reviewed **`481100c584ec772cc2332960ba5d3b28ba36ae6f`**. **Changes required: one P1 blocker, M13-B1.** The exact blocking inputs reported in m1-m12 are fixed, including M12's split-symbol/chelator aliases. A different spelling of that same refused stored tracer alias still passes every refusal stage and draws DARATUMUMAB. This is an inherited, newly demonstrated boundary, not a regression introduced by the latest commit.

No tracked file was edited. New scripts and outputs are confined to `audit_evidence_claims/m13/`; existing brief/log files were preserved. Known over-refusals and performance concerns are reported separately and do not determine the verdict.

- **44 reused name leaf scripts** (from 52 reused scripts) ran with exit 0, covering **62 individual finding rows**, including the original M1 files, cumulative m2b-m12 matrices, **7,511-key** alias sweeps and **1,648-question** frozen no-relink counterfactual. These are same-data method counterfactuals, not whole-deployment comparisons. [Name execution and individual findings](names/FINDINGS.md), [reuse manifest](names/reuse_manifest.json).

## M13-B1 - P1: removing an isotope/chelator separator bypasses every refusal

Exact request: `POST /api/explore` with `{"text":"89ZrDFO-daratumumab"}`.

**Actual:** HTTP 200, centre **DARATUMUMAB / CHEMBL1743007**, matched alias **`89zr-dfo-daratumumab`**, **2 live trials**, no Refused entity or refusal notice. `Which trials evaluate 89ZrDFO-daratumumab?` and `Who sponsors trials of 89ZrDFO-daratumumab?` each return **2 originator evidence rows**. Fully glued `89zrdfodaratumumab` does the same. Appending `, osimertinib` still selects DARATUMUMAB. [Root HTTP evidence](root_checks/independent_m13_http.json), [separate worker adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

**Control and identity proof:** `[89Zr]-DFO-daratumumab`, `89Zr-dfo-daratumumab` and the repaired `[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab` refuse, with no centre and zero qualified trial/sponsor evidence. Bare `daratumumab` works. The leaking spelling and refused control share **canonical key `89zrdfodaratumumab`**, mapped uniquely to the AACT synonym `89zr-dfo-daratumumab` for DARATUMUMAB. Thus the positive lookup recognizes the very stored tracer identity whose refusal is lost; this is not a guess about an unrelated unknown name. [Dictionary evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

| Stage for `89ZrDFO-daratumumab` | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Raw resolver | `ALIAS` to CHEMBL1743007; no blocked component |
| Resolver veto | `{}` |
| Item linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None`; it does not override an existing refusal |
| Public linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Map / trial QA / sponsor QA | Originator centre / 2 evidence rows / 2 evidence rows |

The tested hypothesis is that the latest scan closes split symbols but still relies on input word boundaries. The evidence supports it:

1. Positive lookup deletes separators (`src/textrules.py:339-346`) and matches the complete alias (`src/query.py:413-426`).
2. `_alnum_words` splits only at non-ASCII-letter/digit separators (`src/query.py:111-113`). The candidate produces `['89ZrDFO', 'daratumumab']`; `89Zr` is not a separate word. The new scan at `src/query.py:258-267` glues complete words but never separates an isotope from an already joined word. The first word has seven characters and exceeds its six-character bound.
3. All isotope text is inside the consumed alias, so outside-neighbour checks cannot recover it (`src/query.py:273-289`).
4. The key-prefix fallback isolates `89zr` but requires `dfodaratumumab` to be a same-drug dictionary key (`src/query.py:291-295`). It is absent; only the shorter `daratumumab` key exists. The same failure affects the fully glued alias.
5. Raw tracer predicates also miss this boundary (`src/textrules.py:303-314`; `src/normalize.py:740-745`); the item guard returns false (`src/query.py:119-141`). With all guards silent, `Engine.explore` selects the surviving hit (`src/app.py:87-95`).

Current and exact HEAD^ (`d9260a1ea544cf7bb80415383667784e0aba146a`) query modules both reproduce the leak against the same current app/rules/database. This identifies an **inherited unclosed boundary**, while exact M12-B1 inputs now refuse. [Current/previous comparison](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent comparison](db_ui/adjudication_tracer.json).

**Required outcome:** positive alias normalization must preserve refusal of a stored tracer identity even when the isotope is joined to its chelator or another alias component. Refusal must not require an independently stored isotope-stripped remainder. No fix was implemented in this audit.

`A33124I` is an additional example of the same boundary: stored alias `A33 124i` refuses, but the glued spelling maps to ADAKITUG / CHEMBL5314551 because remainder `a33` is not a standalone key. It has zero trial/sponsor evidence; it is corroboration of M13-B1, not a second counted blocker. [Root HTTP](root_checks/independent_m13_http.json), [dictionary evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

`engine.lock` serializes engine operations (`src/app.py:247-263`), so expensive legal check-answer requests delay map requests. The 3,999/4,000-character stresses are direct-link or **answer-field** cases; `/api/explore` rejects text over 500 characters. Limits precede NFKC normalization (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:116-117,169-177`). No deadlock or invented graph value was observed. The latest six-character word-run loop is bounded per start word (`src/query.py:261-267`); this does not establish linear cost for the surrounding linker.

The new patch showed no material failure in the tested same-data cost comparison. Distinct-piece resolver work, expansion before linking and repeated matched-name scanning remain should-fix costs; they are not used to manufacture a second approval blocker. Owned benchmark port **19403** is closed and lifecycle checks record unchanged DB bytes. No model cost was incurred.

Reproduce with `python -B audit_evidence_claims/m13/cost/run_isolated.py`; its wrappers confine temporary output here and use owned port 19403 sequentially.

## M12-B1 — P1: a chelator-bearing stored tracer alias escapes every refusal

Exact request: `POST /api/explore` with `{"text":"[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab"}`.

**Actual:** HTTP 200, centre **DARATUMUMAB / CHEMBL1743007**, matched alias **`89zr-dfo-daratumumab`**, **2 live trials**, and no Refused entity or refusal notice. Trial and sponsor QA each return **2 originator evidence rows**. `89Z_r-dfo-daratumumab` also leaks. Appending `, osimertinib` still selects DARATUMUMAB. [Independent HTTP](root_checks/independent_m12_http.json), [stage comparison](attacks/focused_results.json).

**Controls:** `89Zr-dfo-daratumumab` and `[89Zr]-DFO-daratumumab` refuse, with zero trial/sponsor evidence. Bare `daratumumab` correctly maps to CHEMBL1743007. Candidate and refused control have the **same canonical key `89zrdfodaratumumab`**, which the index associates with a single AACT synonym for DARATUMUMAB. Thus this is a normalized spelling of the repository's own refused tracer alias, not an unsupported drug guess. [Dictionary/predicate evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json), [independent adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

| Stage | Result for `[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab` |
|---|---|
| Raw resolver | `CONTAINED_ALIAS` to CHEMBL1743007; no blocked component (underscore variant: `ALIAS`) |
| Resolver veto | `{}` |
| Item linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None`; it is not overriding an existing refusal |
| Public linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Map / QA | Originator centre; trial/sponsor evidence 2/2 |

The causal chain is reproducible in the current code:

1. Positive lookup discards punctuation and consumes the full stored alias (`src/query.py:404-417`; `src/textrules.py:339-346`).
2. `_alnum_words` instead produces `['89Z', 'r', 'DFO', 'daratumumab']` (`src/query.py:111-113`). Inside-name isotope checks only inspect one/two-word runs joined by spaces (`src/query.py:258,278-280`). Neither `89Z` nor `89Z r` passes `is_isotope_label`, although glued `89Zr` does (`src/textrules.py:289-298`).
3. The latest glued-neighbour checks inspect words **outside** the consumed alias; there are none containing the isotope (`src/query.py:264-277`).
4. The latest isotope-plus-same-drug-key rule recognizes prefix `89zr`, but requires remainder `dfodaratumumab` to be another same-drug dictionary key (`src/query.py:282-286`). It has no index entry. Bare `daratumumab` does, but the intervening `DFO` remains.
5. The item-wide and raw resolver guards do not reassemble the split element symbol (`src/query.py:125-141`; `src/textrules.py:303-313`; `src/normalize.py:740-745`). `Engine.explore` then selects the surviving positive hit (`src/app.py:87-95`).

**Required outcome:** equivalent punctuation accepted by positive lookup must preserve the refused tracer identity inside a matched stored name, including intervening chelator text. Refusal must not depend on the isotope-stripped remainder being an independently stored alias. Existing lone-name and accepted over-refusal controls should remain explicit. This audit makes no implementation change.

The exact HEAD^ query module also leaks against the same current app/rules/database. **This is an inherited hole, not a regression attributed to this commit.** Only query.py is swapped in that counterfactual; it is not a reconstructed old deployment. [Independent current/previous checks](db_ui/adjudication.md).

## Cost and nonblocking findings

Historical workloads were rerun sequentially after the other CPU-heavy review jobs finished. Unrelated host activity is uncontrolled; timings are observations, not service-level guarantees or proof of global linear complexity. Query-module counterfactuals use the same current database and remaining modules. [Execution](cost/execution_isolated.json), [reuse manifest](reuse_manifest_root.json).

| Reproduction | Current observation / disposition |
|---|---|
| M5-S3 cubic scan, 250 plain tokens | **FIXED for exact old workload:** 2,224 calls per predicate / 33,880 aggregate characters versus old 31,625 / 10,479,750. [Instrumentation](cost/perf_current.json). |
| Original 999-character plain-answer timeout | **FIXED:** check HTTP 200 in **0.22613 s**; ordinary follower **0.03572 s**. [HTTP](cost/perf_check_http.json). |
| M6/M7 repeated 2,000-piece input `';'.join(['x']*2000)` | **FIXED for exact workload:** current direct-link median **0.16853 s**, historical `9bd7cb4` **2.54663 s**; HTTP check **0.47434 s**, queued EGFR **0.39348 s**, both 200. [Comparison](cost/fresh_cost_comparison.json), [HTTP](cost/fresh_cost_http.json). |
| M8-S1 4,097th cache entry | **FIXED:** 82/82 fill requests create 4,096 distinct pieces; first osimertinib at rollover and retry both HTTP200/CHEMBL3353410/79 trials. Original 7,511-key sweep also completes. [HTTP](cost/cache_boundary_http.json). |
| M10/M11 400 distinct drug-bearing pieces, `';'.join(f'isa x{i:04d}' for i in range(400))` | **NOT FIXED**, inherited cost concern: cold check **3.41276 s**, queued EGFR **3.34385 s**, warm check **0.70656 s**. Resolver calls **400 cold / 0 warm**. [HTTP](cost/isolated_unique_cost_http.json), [instrumentation](cost/unique_piece_cost.json). |
| M11 Unicode expansion, answer `'\ufdfa'*4000` | **NOT FIXED:** 4,000 input characters expand to 72,000 at NFKC; check **4.13419 s**, queued EGFR **4.08618 s**, warm **4.16130 s**, all 200. [HTTP](cost/isolated_unicode_cost_http.json). |
| New repeated matches in one item, `' '.join(['isa']*1000)` (3,999 characters) | Nonblocking measured overhead: direct-link three-sample median **1.05735 s** versus HEAD^ **0.63607 s**. At 499 characters: **0.03516 / 0.03013 s**. No failure. [Inputs/comparison](cost/normalization_cost.json). |
| 3,999-character punctuation workload | Current / HEAD^ direct-link medians **0.34400 / 0.37192 s**. Cached ordinary osimertinib **0.000151 / 0.000119 s**. [Comparison](cost/normalization_cost.json). |
| Disease / ordinary concurrency | Disease: 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 per list, **18,575 bytes / 77.053 ms**. 24 requests /12 workers: all expected HTTP200 shapes, **842.766 ms** total. These ordinary samples overlap early audit work. [HTTP](root_checks/http_results.json). |

`engine.lock` serializes engine operations (`src/app.py:247-263`), so expensive legal `/api/check` answers delay maps. The 3,999/4,000-character stresses are direct-link or **answer-field** cases; `/api/explore` correctly rejects text over 500 characters. Limits precede normalization (`src/app.py:239-243,258-263`; `src/query.py:116-117,173-177`). No deadlock or invented graph value was observed.

**M12-S1 — new stored-alias over-refusal, nonblocking.** `rad001c` / `RAD001C` now refuses instead of selecting **EVEROLIMUS / CHEMBL1908360**, whereas the exact HEAD^ query module selects it. This is a stored AACT synonym. The new trailing-key rule divides `rad001c` into same-drug key `rad` and isotope-shaped `001c` (`src/query.py:282-286`; `src/textrules.py:289-298`). Raw resolver still returns ALIAS; no wrong centre is drawn. `rad`, `RAD001`, EVEROLIMUS and its ID still work. [Current/previous proof](names/new_alias_regression.json), [independent HTTP](root_checks/independent_m12_http.json).

The 7,511-key sweep has **70** previous-method positives without a current Drug, versus m11's 69; **`rad001c` is the sole additional loss**. Many existing losses are intended tracer/product refusals, so 70 is not a defect count. The explicitly accepted M5-S2, hydroxyethyl rapamycin, `Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate`, `9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin`, `il-6ra` and `TAK-659F` refusals remain nonblockers. [Loss-set comparison](names/new_alias_losses.json).

Supplementary attacks are recorded without inflating the blocker count: `[89Z.r]-labelled trastuzumab` leaks with 12/12 trial/sponsor rows while `[89Zr]-labelled trastuzumab` refuses; ordinary labelled-distance forms were already exercised and left unpromoted in m11. Punctuated lexical qualifiers and internal-isotope forms on **already labelled** LUTETIUM OXODOTREOTIDE LU-177 are not additional originator blocker claims. [Attack assessment](attacks/FINDINGS.md), [independent adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md). Existing blank-submit, unsupported-name, prefixed comma-alias and failure-closed Infinity/parser limitations are retained in the historical tables.

## Standards axis

**Zero documented-standards findings.** Independent review examined `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD` and the latest query/tests patch. No repository AGENTS.md/CONTRIBUTING/coding-standard source was found. The user-supplied evidence-based, no-tracked-edit and m12-only requirements govern this audit. KOA Studio / KhOpenApi original-versus-modified-code comparison is conditional on application changes; no such changes were made. [Independent standards assessment](db_ui/FINDINGS.md).

## Spec axis

**One P1 blocker, M12-B1.** A normalized spelling of the repository's refused tracer alias maps to its originator, with every refusal stage missing it. The exact m11 repairs work, but their same-drug remainder requirement leaves chelator-bearing aliases exposed. Stored-edge, stored-value, direction, cut-notice, no-model and exercised UI safety/state checks pass. Correct relationships around a wrongly chosen centre do not satisfy the refusal guarantee. Accepted over-refusals and cost observations do not determine the verdict. [Independent specification assessment](attacks/FINDINGS.md), [adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

## Integrity and reproduction

Snapshot: **7 retired nodes, 142 retired edges, zero real self-loops and zero live edges with retired endpoints**. All six live node types and all 22 existing group combinations were exercised. Self-loop/unknown-type behavior was tested with an isolated in-memory fixture. [Snapshot](db_ui/db_snapshot_facts.json), [fixture](db_ui/self_loop_unknown.json).

`Engine.ask` uses `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). Explore follows SELECT/read paths; mutation fixtures use memory copies. The application does not globally enforce a read-only SQLite handle, so this is an executed-path/source/hash result, not a database-permission guarantee. Final verification checks **3,731 tracked-file SHA-256 hashes**, HEAD, tracked/staged diffs, DB bytes, report links and owned-port cleanup. [Initial snapshot](integrity_before.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json). DB SHA-256: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`. Existing m12 brief/log files were preserved.

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/setup_root.py
"""Preserve tracked bytes; adapt historical audit scripts, never their evidence."""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib, json, subprocess

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
tracked = subprocess.check_output(['git', 'ls-files', '-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8').split('\0')
initial = {
    'head': subprocess.check_output(['git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD'], cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),
    'tracked_sha256': {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in tracked if p},
    'status': subprocess.check_output(['git', 'status', '--porcelain'], cwd=ROOT).decode(),
    'db_sha256': hashlib.sha256((ROOT / 'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
}
(OUT / 'integrity_before.json').write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
manifest = []
for sub in ['root_checks', 'cost']:
    dest = OUT / sub
    (dest / 'tmp').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    for src in sorted((ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m12' / sub).glob('*.py')):
        if src.name in ('setup.py', 'build_report.py'):
            continue
        code = src.read_text(encoding='utf8')
        changes = []
        for old, new in [('8917', '19400'), ('8918', '19401'), ('8919', '19402'), ('8914', '19403')]:
            if old in code:
                code = code.replace(old, new)
                changes.append(f'port {old} -> {new}')
        assert 'audit_evidence_claims/m12/' not in code, src
        (dest / src.name).write_text(code, encoding='utf8')
        manifest.append({'source': src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), 'copy': (dest/src.name).relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
                         'sha256': hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), 'changes': changes,
                         'output_root': '__file__ relative m13; historical inputs unchanged'})
(OUT / 'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({'tracked': len(initial['tracked_sha256']), 'scripts': len(manifest), 'head': initial['head']}))


FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/root_checks/run_all.py
"""Sequential test and real-HTTP reproduction suite."""
from pathlib import Path
import json,os,subprocess,sys,time
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1',PYTHONUTF8='1',PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
jobs=[['run_replays.py','tests'],['run_replays.py','http'],['run_remaining.py'],['new_leaks_http.py']]
rows=[]
for args in jobs:
 start=time.perf_counter()
 p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/args[0]),*args[1:]],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=900)
 (OUT/(args[0]+'.batch.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
 row=dict(args=args,exit_code=p.returncode,seconds=time.perf_counter()-start);rows.append(row)
 (OUT/'execution_all.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
 print(json.dumps(row),flush=True)
 if p.returncode:raise SystemExit(p.returncode)


FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/root_checks/run_verification.py
"""Run the unmodified existing suite with scratch confined to this directory."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import time

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
TMP = OUT / "tmp"
TMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP": str(TMP), "TEMP": str(TMP),
       "TMPDIR": str(TMP)}

def git(*args):
    result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

files = ["src/app.py", "src/query.py", "web/try.html", "tests/test_app.py",
         "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"]
before = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
(OUT / "verification_initial.json").write_text(json.dumps({"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
    "status": git("status", "--short"), "hashes": before}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]
start = time.perf_counter()
run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True)
(OUT / "pytest.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
after = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
summary = {"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider",
           "environment": {k: env[k] for k in ("PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE", "PYTHONUTF8", "PYTHONIOENCODING", "TMP", "TEMP", "TMPDIR")},
           "exit_code": run.returncode, "wall_seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - start, 3),
           "hashes_unchanged": before == after, "hashes_after": after}
(OUT / "pytest_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print((run.stdout + run.stderr).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
raise SystemExit(run.returncode)


FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/cost/run_all.py
"""Run copied historical workloads and Round11 controls sequentially."""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib,json,os,subprocess,sys,time
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[2]

  (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
  row={'script':name,'exit_code':p.returncode,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start}
 except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
  (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes((e.stdout or b'')+(e.stderr or b''))
  row={'script':name,'timeout_seconds':240,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start}
 rows.append(row);(OUT/'execution.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8');print(json.dumps(row),flush=True)
 if row.get('exit_code')!=0:raise SystemExit(1)

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/finalize_integrity.py
"""Final byte, path, lifecycle and report-link checks; never edit application files."""
from pathlib import Path
import datetime,hashlib,json,re,socket,subprocess
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[1]
before=json.loads((OUT/'integrity_before.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
def git(*args):
 p=subprocess.run(['git',*args],cwd=ROOT,capture_output=True)
 return dict(code=p.returncode,stdout=p.stdout.decode('utf8','replace'),stderr=p.stderr.decode('utf8','replace'))
changed=[];missing=[]
for rel,digest in before['tracked_sha256'].items():
 p=ROOT/rel
 if not p.exists():missing.append(rel)
 elif hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=digest:changed.append(rel)
tracked=set(git('ls-files','-z')['stdout'].split('\0'))-{''}
ports={}
for port in [19300,19330,19331,19360,19361,19400,19401,19402,19403,19404]:
 with socket.socket() as sock:
  sock.settimeout(.2);ports[str(port)]=sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',port))!=0
report=(OUT/'REPORT.md').read_text(encoding='utf8')
links=re.findall(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)',report)
broken=[s for s in links if s!='integrity_final.json' and not (OUT/s.split('#',1)[0]).exists()]
db=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest()
status=git('status','--short')
result=dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),head_before=before['head'],head_after=git('rev-parse','HEAD')['stdout'].strip(),
 tracked_files_hashed=len(before['tracked_sha256']),changed_tracked_files=changed,missing_tracked_files=missing,
 new_tracked_paths=sorted(tracked-set(before['tracked_sha256'])),tracked_diff=git('diff','--exit-code'),staged_diff=git('diff','--cached','--exit-code'),
 git_status=status,initial_status=before['status'],database_sha256_before=before['db_sha256'],database_sha256_after=db,database_unchanged=db==before['db_sha256'],
 owned_ports_closed=ports,report_links_checked=len(links),broken_report_links=broken,report_final_line=report.rstrip().splitlines()[-1],
 report_sha256=hashlib.sha256((OUT/'REPORT.md').read_bytes()).hexdigest())
result['passed']=(not changed and not missing and not result['new_tracked_paths'] and result['head_before']==result['head_after']
 and result['tracked_diff']['code']==result['staged_diff']['code']==0 and result['database_unchanged'] and all(ports.values())
 and not broken and status['stdout']==before['status'] and result['report_final_line'] in ('VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED', 'VERDICT: APPROVE'))
(OUT/'integrity_final.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
assert result['passed'],'Final integrity verification failed'

## M13-B1 - P1: removing an isotope/chelator separator bypasses every refusal

Exact request: `POST /api/explore` with `{"text":"89ZrDFO-daratumumab"}`.

**Actual:** HTTP 200, centre **DARATUMUMAB / CHEMBL1743007**, matched alias **`89zr-dfo-daratumumab`**, **2 live trials**, no Refused entity or refusal notice. `Which trials evaluate 89ZrDFO-daratumumab?` and `Who sponsors trials of 89ZrDFO-daratumumab?` each return **2 originator evidence rows**. Fully glued `89zrdfodaratumumab` does the same. Appending `, osimertinib` still selects DARATUMUMAB. [Root HTTP evidence](root_checks/independent_m13_http.json), [separate worker adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

**Control and identity proof:** `[89Zr]-DFO-daratumumab`, `89Zr-dfo-daratumumab` and the repaired `[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab` refuse, with no centre and zero qualified trial/sponsor evidence. Bare `daratumumab` works. The leaking spelling and refused control share **canonical key `89zrdfodaratumumab`**, mapped uniquely to the AACT synonym `89zr-dfo-daratumumab` for DARATUMUMAB. Thus the positive lookup recognizes the very stored tracer identity whose refusal is lost; this is not a guess about an unrelated unknown name. [Dictionary evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

| Stage for `89ZrDFO-daratumumab` | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Raw resolver | `ALIAS` to CHEMBL1743007; no blocked component |
| Resolver veto | `{}` |
| Item linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None`; it does not override an existing refusal |
| Public linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Map / trial QA / sponsor QA | Originator centre / 2 evidence rows / 2 evidence rows |

The tested hypothesis is that the latest scan closes split symbols but still relies on input word boundaries. The evidence supports it:

1. Positive lookup deletes separators (`src/textrules.py:339-346`) and matches the complete alias (`src/query.py:413-426`).
2. `_alnum_words` splits only at non-ASCII-letter/digit separators (`src/query.py:111-113`). The candidate produces `['89ZrDFO', 'daratumumab']`; `89Zr` is not a separate word. The new scan at `src/query.py:258-267` glues complete words but never separates an isotope from an already joined word. The first word has seven characters and exceeds its six-character bound.
3. All isotope text is inside the consumed alias, so outside-neighbour checks cannot recover it (`src/query.py:273-289`).
4. The key-prefix fallback isolates `89zr` but requires `dfodaratumumab` to be a same-drug dictionary key (`src/query.py:291-295`). It is absent; only the shorter `daratumumab` key exists. The same failure affects the fully glued alias.
5. Raw tracer predicates also miss this boundary (`src/textrules.py:303-314`; `src/normalize.py:740-745`); the item guard returns false (`src/query.py:119-141`). With all guards silent, `Engine.explore` selects the surviving hit (`src/app.py:87-95`).

Current and exact HEAD^ (`d9260a1ea544cf7bb80415383667784e0aba146a`) query modules both reproduce the leak against the same current app/rules/database. This identifies an **inherited unclosed boundary**, while exact M12-B1 inputs now refuse. [Current/previous comparison](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent comparison](db_ui/adjudication_tracer.json).

**Required outcome:** positive alias normalization must preserve refusal of a stored tracer identity even when the isotope is joined to its chelator or another alias component. Refusal must not require an independently stored isotope-stripped remainder. No fix was implemented in this audit.

`A33124I` is an additional example of the same boundary: stored alias `A33 124i` refuses, but the glued spelling maps to ADAKITUG / CHEMBL5314551 because remainder `a33` is not a standalone key. It has zero trial/sponsor evidence; it is corroboration of M13-B1, not a second counted blocker. [Root HTTP](root_checks/independent_m13_http.json), [dictionary evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

`engine.lock` serializes engine operations (`src/app.py:247-263`), so expensive legal check-answer requests delay map requests. The 3,999/4,000-character stresses are direct-link or **answer-field** cases; `/api/explore` rejects text over 500 characters. Limits precede NFKC normalization (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:116-117,169-177`). No deadlock or invented graph value was observed. The latest six-character word-run loop is bounded per start word (`src/query.py:261-267`); this does not establish linear cost for the surrounding linker.

The new patch showed no material failure in the tested same-data cost comparison. Distinct-piece resolver work, expansion before linking and repeated matched-name scanning remain should-fix costs; they are not used to manufacture a second approval blocker. Owned benchmark port **19403** is closed and lifecycle checks record unchanged DB bytes. No model cost was incurred.

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/root_checks/run_replays.py
"""Execute inherited reproducers, owning and closing one HTTP port at a time."""
import json,os,subprocess,sys,time
from pathlib import Path
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
env={**os.environ,'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE':'1','PYTHONUTF8':'1','PYTHONIOENCODING':'utf-8',
     'TMP':str(OUT/'tmp'),'TEMP':str(OUT/'tmp'),'TMPDIR':str(OUT/'tmp')}
names=(['run_verification.py'] if 'tests' in sys.argv else ['perf_probe.py'] if 'perf' in sys.argv
       else ['run_http.py','supplemental_http.py','attack_http.py','m5_http.py','cross_item_http.py','perf_check_http.py'])
rows=[]
for name in names:
    start=time.perf_counter()
    p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/name)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=240)
    (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)

    (OUT/('execution_'+sys.argv[-1]+'.json')).write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
    print(json.dumps(rows[-1]),flush=True)

FILE src/query.py
70: 
71:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
72:         return {
73:             "question": self.question,
74:             "intent": self.intent,
75:             "entities": self.entities,
76:             "answer": self.text,
77:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
78:             "checks": self.checks,
79:             "evidence": [
80:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
81:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
82:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,

87: 
88: 
89: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
90: # engine
91: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
92: # Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
93: # them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
94: # nanoparticles").
95: _JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
96:             "or/and", "and/or"}
97: 
98: 
99: 
100: # every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
101: _DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
102:                         if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"
103: 
104: 
105: def _plain(text: str) -> str:
106:     """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
107:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
108:     return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))
109: 
110: 
111: def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
112:     """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
113:     return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))
114: 
115: 
116: def _fold(text: str) -> str:
117:     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))
118: 
119: 
120: def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
121:     """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").
122: 
123:     A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
124:     dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
125:     for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
126:         for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
127:             # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
128:             if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
129:                 continue
130:             if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
131:                 continue
132:             elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
133:             if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
134:                 return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
135:             if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
136:                                                       else rest[-1:].isupper()):
137:                 return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
138:     return False
139: 
140: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
141:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
142: 
143:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
144:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
145:     work grows linearly with the item."""
146:     def hit(text: str) -> bool:
147:         # the alias key ignores punctuation, underscores and accents, so the guards also
148:         # see the text with those folded ("bevacizumab-(awwb)", "bevacizumab_awwb",
149:         # "bevácizumab-awwb")
150:         plain = _plain(text)
151:         # and with every separator (punctuation, underscore, spaces) read as one space,
152:         # so "generic-version of", "albumin - bound" meet the multiword patterns
153:         return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
154:                    for v in {text, plain, re.sub(r"(?:[^\w\s]|_)+", "-", plain).strip("-"),
155:                              " ".join(_alnum_words(plain))})
156: 
157:     if hit(" ".join(words)):
158:         return True
159:     if any(T.canonical_key(w) in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES for w in words):
160:         return True
161:     return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
162:                for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))
163: 
164: class OncoKG:
165:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
166:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
167:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
168:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
169:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
170:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
171:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
172:         self._resolve_cache: dict = {}
173:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
174:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
175:                         if t.get("symbol")}
176:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
177:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
178:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
179:         for row in self.conn.execute(
180:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
181:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
182:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
183:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
184:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
185:             if same in self.companies:
186:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
187:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
188: 
189:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
190:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
191:         # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
192:         question = _fold(question)
193:         blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
194:         found = self._link(question)
195:         whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked, found)
196:         if whole:
197:             return [whole]
198:         if not blocked:
199:             return found
200:         # a drug the resolver refuses in this text is refused here too, whatever the
201:         # splitting below made of it (the two must agree before anything is linked)
202:         out = [e for e in found if not (e["type"] == "Drug" and e["id"] in blocked)]
203:         for text in sorted({t for t in blocked.values()}):
204:             if not any(e["type"] == "Refused" and (text in e["label"] or e["label"] in text)
205:                        for e in out):
206:                 out.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text, "matched_text": text,
207:                             "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not the "
208:                                    "originator molecule"})
209:         return out
210: 
211:     def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
212:         """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
213:         product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
214:         comps = []
215:         lone = set()
216:         pieces = {p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001/|]", text) if p.strip()}
217:         for piece in pieces:                         # each distinct list piece once
218:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[^\w\-]+", piece) if w]
219:             if not self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
220:                 continue                             # no drug in it: nothing to refuse
221:             got = self._resolve_cache.get(piece)
222:             if got is None:
223:                 try:
224:                     got = N.resolve_drug_string(piece, self.dic, self.index_keys)["components"]
225:                 except Exception:                    # never let a guard crash a lookup
226:                     got = []
227:                 if len(self._resolve_cache) >= 4096:
228:                     self._resolve_cache.clear()
229:                 self._resolve_cache[piece] = got
230:             if len(words) == 1 and T.canonical_key(piece) in self.dic["index"]:
231:                 lone.add(piece)                      # one stored name on its own ("566C")
232:             comps += got
233:         out: dict = {}
234:         accepted = {c.get("drug_id") for c in comps
235:                     if c.get("drug_id") and not str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED")}
236:         for c in comps:
237:             piece = c.get("component") or ""
238:             tracer = "radiotracer_guard" in (c.get("rules") or []) or T.is_radiotracer(piece)
239:             if not (c.get("tier") == "BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT"
240:                     or (str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED") and tracer)):
241:                 continue
242:             if piece in lone:
243:                 continue
244:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[\s/|,;]+", piece) if w]
245:             words = [w.strip(" ?.!:()[]") or w for w in words]
246:             for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
247:                 key = T.canonical_key(" ".join(words[a:b]))
248:                 for h in self.dic["index"].get(key, []):
249:                     # the same molecule named separately and accepted ("biosimilar
250:                     # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below
251:                     if h["drug_id"] not in accepted:
252:                         out[h["drug_id"]] = piece
253:         return out
254: 
255:     def _keys_of(self, did: str) -> set:
256:         """Every lookup key that names this drug."""
257:         if not hasattr(self, "_drug_keys"):
258:             self._drug_keys: dict = {}
259:             for k, hits in self.dic["index"].items():
260:                 for h in hits:
261:                     self._drug_keys.setdefault(h["drug_id"], set()).add(k)
262:         return self._drug_keys.get(did, set())
263: 
264:     def _marked(self, key: str, did: str, tokens, seg, a: int, b: int):
265:         """The text that makes this drug name a distinct product or a tracer, if any.
266: 
267:         Checked on the same letters-and-digits form the name was looked up by: a known
268:         biosimilar suffix at the end of the name ("bevacizumab awwb", "bevacizumab_awwb")
269:         or as the next word, or an isotope label just before or after it ("[89.Zr]
270:         trastuzumab", "89:Zr trastuzumab")."""
271:         for sfx in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES:
272:             stem = key[:-len(sfx)]
273:             if key.endswith(sfx) and stem and any(
274:                     h["drug_id"] == did for h in self.dic["index"].get(stem, [])):
275:                 return " ".join(tokens[a:b])
276:         same = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == seg[a]]
277:         before = [k for k in same if k < a]
278:         after = [k for k in same if k >= b]
279:         # words counted after normalisation, so punctuation never uses up the budget
280:         prev = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-2:]
281:         nxt = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:2]
282:         own = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))
283:         lone = not before and not after      # the name is the whole list item
284:         # an isotope label anywhere in the name ("A33 124i"), or just before or after it
285:         # ("[89.Zr] trastuzumab", "trastuzumab 89 - Zr"); an isotope-shaped name counts
286:         # as a name only when it stands alone ("566C", "18f"), never as the substance
287:         # of a longer phrase ("18F fluorothymidine")
288:         inside = [own[i:j] for i in range(len(own)) for j in (i + 1, i + 2) if j <= len(own)]
289:         # and every run of the name's words glued as the key glues them, so a symbol
290:         # split by punctuation inside a stored alias is still an isotope ("[89Z.r]-DFO-")
291:         for i in range(len(own)):
292:             for j in range(i + 1, len(own) + 1):
293:                 glued = "".join(own[i:j])
294:                 if len(glued) > 6:
295:                     break
296:                 if j - i > 1:
297:                     inside.append([glued])
298:         if lone and T.is_isotope_label("".join(own)):
299:             inside = []                      # the whole item is one isotope-shaped name
300:         name = " ".join(tokens[a:b])
301:         # the neighbours glued back together the way the lookup key glues them, so a
302:         # suffix or an isotope spelt in pieces is still seen ("a - w w b", "[89Z.r]")
303:         nxt_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:8]
304:         prev_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-8:]
305:         for n in range(1, len(nxt_all) + 1):
306:             glued = "".join(nxt_all[:n]).lower()
307:             if glued in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES or T.is_isotope_label(glued):
308:                 return f"{name} ({' '.join(nxt_all[:n])})"
309:             if len(glued) > 6:
310:                 break
311:         for n in range(1, len(prev_all) + 1):
312:             glued = "".join(prev_all[-n:]).lower()
313:             if T.is_isotope_label(glued):
314:                 return f"({' '.join(prev_all[-n:])}) {name}"
315:             if len(glued) > 6:
316:                 break
317:         # a label fused to a neighbouring word, such as a chelator ("DFO89Zr", "89ZrDFO")
318:         for w in nxt_all[:2] + prev_all[-2:]:
319:             if _isotope_edge(w):
320:                 return f"{name} ({w})"
321:         for ws in inside + [prev[-1:], prev, nxt[:1], nxt]:
322:             if ws and T.is_isotope_label(" ".join(ws)):
323:                 return name if ws in inside else f"{name} ({' '.join(ws)})"
324:         # an isotope glued to the name itself ("89zr-trastuzumab" read through "[89Z.r]")
325:         # anything may sit between them (a chelator: "89ZrDFO-daratumumab"), so the rest
326:         # needs only to end, or start, with a name of the same drug
327:         # A one-letter element is left to the case-marked word check above: lower-case
328:         # chemistry ("13-cis retinoic acid") is not carbon-13.
329:         names = [k for k in self._keys_of(did) if len(k) >= 5]
330:         for i in range(3, min(6, len(key))):
331:             head, tail = key[:i], key[i:]
332:             if (tail and T.is_isotope_label(head) and head[-2:].isalpha()
333:                     and any(tail.endswith(k) for k in names)):
334:                 return name
335:             head, tail = key[-i:], key[:-i]
336:             if (tail and T.is_isotope_label(head) and head[-2:].isalpha()
337:                     and any(tail.startswith(k) for k in names)):
338:                 return name
339: 
340:         return None
341: 
342:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
343:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
344:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
345: 
346:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
347:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
348:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
349:             return None
350:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/root_checks/independent_m13_http.py
"""Independent CLI HTTP confirmation of candidate round-nine findings."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
PORT = 19404
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
CASES = ['89ZrDFO-daratumumab', '89zrdfodaratumumab', '89ZrDFO-daratumumab, osimertinib', '[89Zr]-DFO-daratumumab', '89Zr-dfo-daratumumab', '[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab', '89Z_r-dfo-daratumumab', 'daratumumab', 'osimertinib', 'A33124I', 'A33 124i', 'ADAKITUG', 'rad001c', 'RAD001', 'EVEROLIMUS']


def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"}
with (OUT / "independent_m13_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m13_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m13_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")


FILE audit_evidence_claims/m13/root_checks/fresh_probe.py
"""Independent small normalization attack matrix, read-only Engine calls."""
from pathlib import Path
import sys,json
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0,str(ROOT/'src'))
from app import Engine
import query as Q
import textrules as T
engine=Engine()
engine.kg.conn.execute('PRAGMA query_only=ON')
cases=[
 '[89Z.r] trastuzumab','89Z_r trastuzumab','trastuzumab [89Z.r]',
 '[89Zr] trastuzumab','89 Zr trastuzumab','[8.9Zr] trastuzumab',
 'trastuzumab 8 9 Zr','bevacizumab - a - w - w - b',
 'bevacizumab a w w b','bevacizumab - awwb','bevacizumab a.w.w.b',
 'trastuzumab labelled with 89Zr','trastuzumab labeled with 89Zr',
 '89Zr labeled trastuzumab','89Zr conjugated trastuzumab',
 '99MTc trastuzumab','[99MTc] trastuzumab','99mTc trastuzumab',
 'trastuzumab','bevacizumab','osimertinib',
]
rows=[]
for text in cases:
    folded=Q._fold(text)
    raw=engine.resolve(text)
    blocked=engine.kg._resolver_blocked(folded)
    item=engine.kg._link(folded)
    whole=engine.kg._whole_name(folded,blocked,item)
    public=engine.kg.link_entities(text)
    mapped=engine.explore(text)
    rows.append(dict(input=text,key=T.canonical_key(text),words=Q._alnum_words(text),
                     isotope_predicate=T.is_isotope_label(text),raw=raw,blocked=blocked,
                     item=item,whole=whole,public=public,map=mapped))
    print(json.dumps(dict(input=text,center=mapped.get('center'),refused=mapped.get('refused')),ensure_ascii=True),flush=True)
(OUT/'fresh_probe.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
engine.kg.conn.close()

--- a/src/query.py
+++ b/src/query.py
@@ -116,6 +116,27 @@ def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
 def _fold(text: str) -> str:
     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))
 
+
+def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
+    """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").
+
+    A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
+    dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
+    for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
+        for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
+            # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
+            if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
+                continue
+            if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
+                continue
+            elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
+            if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
+                return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
+            if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
+                                                      else rest[-1:].isupper()):
+                return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
+    return False
+

src/textrules.py
288: # and the substance they label is usually not in a therapeutic vocabulary at all.
289: RE_ISOTOPE = re.compile(r"^\[?(\d{1,3}m?)[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?$")
290: ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS = {
291:     "f", "c", "n", "o", "ga", "cu", "zr", "i", "lu", "tc", "in", "y", "at",
292:     "ac", "pb", "re", "sm", "sr", "ra", "rb", "br", "h", "p", "s",
293: }
294: 
295: 
296: def is_isotope_label(text: str) -> bool:
297:     m = RE_ISOTOPE.match((text or "").strip())
298:     return bool(m and m.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)
299: 
300: 
301: # "[18F]F-AraG", "68Ga-DOTATATE": an isotope prefix marks the whole string as a
302: # radiolabelled tracer, whose unlabelled moiety is not the administered drug.
303: RE_RADIOTRACER = re.compile(
304:     r"(?:^|[\s\(\[])\[?\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]?\s*[-–]\s*\w"
305:     r"|(?:^|[\s\(])\[\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]\s*[A-Za-z]", re.I)
306: 
307: 
308: def is_radiotracer(text: str) -> bool:
309:     t = (text or "").strip()
310:     m = RE_RADIOTRACER.search(t)
311:     if not m:
312:         return False
313:     head = re.match(r"^[\s\(\[]*\[?(\d{1,3})m?[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?", t)
314:     return bool(head and head.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)
315: 
316: 
317: def is_dev_code(text: str) -> bool:
318:     """True for alphanumeric development codes, where fuzzy matching is unsafe."""
319:     t = (text or "").strip()
320:     return bool(RE_DEV_CODE.match(t))
321: 
322: 
323: def dev_code_core(text: str) -> str | None:
324:     """Strip a trailing lot/format number: 'BMS-936558-01' -> 'BMS-936558'."""
325:     m = RE_DEV_CODE_SUFFIX.match((text or "").strip())
326:     return m.group("core") if m else None
327: 
328: 
329: def _strip_words(text: str, words: list[str]) -> tuple[str, bool]:
330:     fired = False
331:     for w in sorted(words, key=len, reverse=True):
332:         pattern = re.compile(rf"\b{re.escape(w)}\b", re.I)
333:         if pattern.search(text):
334:             text = pattern.sub(" ", text)
335:             fired = True
336:     return text, fired
337: 
338: 
339: def canonical_key(text: str) -> str:
340:     """Aggressive key used for dictionary lookup (never shown to a user)."""
341:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
342:     t = "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c))
343:     t = t.lower()
344:     t = t.replace("’", "'").replace("‐", "-").replace("–", "-").replace("—", "-")
345:     t = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "", t)
346:     return t
347:

# Round 14 independent originator attacks

Reviewed HEAD `73ee310` against the current database. **Two P1 blockers:** a newly introduced one-letter-isotope regression, and an inherited two-letter-isotope alias whose trailing imaging suffix defeats the revised key check. Every refusal stage misses both. The exact M13 DARATUMUMAB reproduction is fixed.

## M14-B1 — a consumed one-letter-isotope alias bypasses the case-marked check (regression)

**Exact HTTP:** `POST /api/explore`, JSON `{"text":"3HVINBLASTINE"}`. HTTP 200 draws **VINBLASTINE / CHEMBL159**, matched alias **`[3h]-vinblastine`**, with **5 live trials**, and no refusal. `Which trials evaluate 3HVINBLASTINE?` and `Who sponsors trials of 3HVINBLASTINE?` each return **5 originator evidence rows**. `3HVinblastine`, `3Hvinblastine`, and `3hvinblastine` do the same. With `3Hvinblastine, osimertinib`, the wrong first centre is still VINBLASTINE. [Full stages and previous comparison](edge_results.json), [HTTP](edge_http.json).

**Control and identity:** `[3h]-vinblastine` and `[3H]vinblastine` are refused, with zero trial/sponsor evidence. Their canonical key is the same **`3hvinblastine`** as the accepted input, uniquely naming the stored tracer alias of VINBLASTINE. Bare `vinblastine` remains available. For the deliberately case-marked input **`3HVINBLASTINE`**, `_isotope_edge` returns **true**. Thus this finding does not depend on reading lower-case chemistry as an isotope. [Dictionary, helper result, key checks](dictionary_evidence.json).

The HEAD^ query module refuses all four glued spellings and selects the separate OSIMERTINIB in the comma control. This is a **current-patch regression**, established with the same current app, normalization/rules, and database and only `query.py` replaced in memory. It is not a reconstructed old deployment. [Version-labelled direct results](edge_results.json).

**Cause:** positive matching consumes the complete alias (`src/query.py:450-470`). The new helper is called only on **outside neighbouring words** (`src/query.py:317-320`), never on the consumed name's `own` words. The inside scan (`src/query.py:282-297,321-323`) recognizes whole isotope labels but cannot split `3HVINBLASTINE`. The revised key fallback excludes one-letter elements and starts its prefix-length loop at 3 (`src/query.py:327-337`), although `3H` has length 2. The previous key fallback refused this canonical alias. The comment saying that one-letter elements are left to the case-marked check is ineffective for a consumed alias.

**Required outcome:** preserve refusal for a known tracer alias when its isotope separator is removed, including the case-marked form the new helper already recognizes, without broadening lower-case dose/chemistry interpretation.

## M14-B2 — a two-letter-isotope alias with a trailing PET/CT marker still maps its originator (inherited)

**Exact HTTP:** `POST /api/explore`, JSON `{"text":"89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT"}`. HTTP 200 draws **PANITUMUMAB / CHEMBL1201827**, matched alias **`89zr-panitumumab-pet/ct`**, with **8 live trials**. Trial and sponsor QA return **8 / 7 originator evidence rows**. There is no Refused entity or refusal notice. [HTTP](edge_http.json), [stages](edge_results.json).

**Minimal control:** **`89Zr-panitumumab-PET-CT`** refuses and yields zero QA evidence. Removing only the hyphen between the isotope and the drug causes the leak. Both strings have canonical key **`89zrpanitumumabpetct`**, uniquely pointing to the same stored AACT alias. `89Zr panitumumab PET CT` also refuses; bare `panitumumab` still works. Fully glued `89ZrpanitumumabPETCT`, lower-case `89zrpanitumumabpetct`, and `89Zrpanitumumab PET CT` leak too. Appending `, osimertinib` does not prevent the wrong PANITUMUMAB centre. [Dictionary evidence](dictionary_evidence.json), [compact matrix](edge_summary.json).

**Cause:** the isotope is fused inside a consumed alias, so it is neither a whole isotope word nor an outside neighbour (`src/query.py:282-323`). `_isotope_edge('89Zrpanitumumab')` is true, but this helper is never applied to that own word. The final fallback isolates `89zr` but requires the remaining key **to end with** a same-drug key of at least five characters (`src/query.py:329-334`). `panitumumabpetct` does not end in any such key: the stored imaging marker follows the drug name. Allowing arbitrary text only **between** the isotope and a terminal same-drug name does not protect this actual stored tracer alias. The HEAD^ query module has the same leak, so this is inherited, not a newly introduced regression.

**Required outcome:** normalization of a stored refused tracer alias must preserve its refused identity even when the alias includes a trailing imaging marker.

## Every stage for both blockers

| Stage | `3HVINBLASTINE` | `89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT` |
|---|---|---|
| Raw intervention resolver | ALIAS / CHEMBL159 | ALIAS / CHEMBL1201827 |
| `_qualified` | false | false |
| Resolver veto | `{}` | `{}` |
| Item linker | Drug; no Refused | Drug; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None` | `None` |
| Public linker | VINBLASTINE; no Refused | PANITUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Map centre / live trial count | CHEMBL159 / 5 | CHEMBL1201827 / 8 |
| Qualified trial / sponsor QA rows | 5 / 5 | 8 / 7 |

The raw tracer predicate expects a boundary after the element (`src/textrules.py:303-314`), so the normalized raw resolver also misses the guard (`src/normalize.py:738-746`). `Engine.explore` selects the surviving first positive hit (`src/app.py:88-95`). These are not cases where the map ignores an already-recorded refusal; **all refusal paths miss**. The relationships shown may be valid for the originator but are attached to a wrongly selected centre.

This also passes the raw resolver veto and item predicate. It is retained as a supplementary boundary observation; the two stored-alias blockers above independently decide the approval bar. [Direct neighbouring-word probes](extra_neighbors.json).

- The final HTTP evidence contains **308 requests**: 76 reused M12, 108 reused M13, and 124 new/control requests. All return HTTP 200; refusal correctness is checked from response bodies.
- Owned ports **8950, 8951, 8952** are closed. All CLI servers ran unmodified `src/app.py`; served page bytes match `web/try.html`; the database SHA-256 was unchanged. [M12 lifecycle](new_leaks_lifecycle.json), [M13 lifecycle](fresh_lifecycle.json), [new lifecycle](edge_lifecycle.json).
- Comparison probes reopen SQLite with `mode=ro`. No mutation fixtures, application edits, or model calls were used. Bytecode is disabled; HTTP temporary directories and all new scripts/results remain inside m14/attacks.
- Timings overlap other review work and are not benchmarks. The parent review owns historical-name completeness, demo/UI/DB checks, cost testing, pytest, and whole-worktree integrity.

Reproduce from repository root: `python -B audit_evidence_claims/m14/attacks/run.py`. Servers check their own ports and terminate only the processes they start.

    times = []

        entities = kg.link_entities(value)
        times.append(time.perf_counter() - start)
    detail = {}
    if ref == "HEAD":
        for name in ["_resolver_blocked", "_link"]:
            start = time.perf_counter()
            result = getattr(kg, name)(value)
            detail[name] = {"seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "result_count": len(result)}
    kg.conn.close()
    print(json.dumps({"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "characters": len(value),
                      "times": times, "median": statistics.median(times),
                      "entities": entities, "stages": detail}))

'ﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺ 'ﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺﷺ +"""Assemble this round's report from fresh, independently inspected evidence."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import json
+import posixpath
+import re
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+
+def read(rel):
+    return (OUT / rel).read_text(encoding="utf8")
+
+def data(rel):
+    return json.loads(read(rel))
+
+def links(text, prefix):
+    return re.sub(r"\]\(([^)]+)\)", lambda m: "](" + posixpath.normpath(prefix + "/" + m.group(1)) + ")", text)
+
+attack = read("attacks/FINDINGS.md")
+blockers = attack.split("## M14-B1", 1)[1].split("## Reused earlier attacks and controls", 1)[0]
+blockers = links("## M14-B1" + blockers, "attacks")
+names = read("names/FINDINGS.md").split("## Individual historical dispositions", 1)[1].split("## Latest repair and remaining limits", 1)[0]
+names = links(names, "names")
+db = read("db_ui/FINDINGS.md").split("## Historical findings: individual dispositions", 1)[1].split("## Standards axis", 1)[0]
+db = links(db, "db_ui")
+
+norm = {(r["ref"], r["name"]): r for r in data("cost/normalization_cost.json")}
+def median(ref, name):
+    return f'{norm[ref, name]["median_seconds"]:.5f}'
+unique = data("cost/isolated_unique_cost_http.json")
+unicode = data("cost/isolated_unicode_cost_http.json")
+oldnew = data("cost/fresh_cost_comparison.json")
+split = {r["ref"]: r["median"] for r in oldnew if r["kind"] == "split" and r["size"] == 2000}
+repeat = data("cost/fresh_cost_http.json")
+plain = data("cost/perf_check_http.json")
+http = data("root_checks/http_results.json")
+perf = next(r for r in data("cost/perf_current.json") if r["kind"] == "plain" and r["size"] == 250)
+head = data("integrity_before.json")["head"]
+assert len(data("names/historical_name_dispositions.json")) == 64
+assert len(norm) == 24
+assert all(r["exit_code"] == 0 for r in data("cost/execution_isolated.json"))
+
